The accepted cost is wall-clock at the §5.2 politeness floor of 1 req/s per host with 2 hosts in flight, and **no LLM spend whatsoever** — Phase 2 has never run against this corpus. **DURABLE was weighed and rejected on §8 rather than on convenience.** A durable home means a second copy of a database holding the names, addresses and — after M5 — the contact details of 13 real businesses, in a location outside the container and therefore outside the ruling M3 already took when it made this repository private. Errors are asymmetric and the two directions are not comparable: the disposable error costs this project a re-crawl and the measurements in M1.97, while the durable error puts third-party personal data somewhere §8's handling rules do not reach, on behalf of people who never consented to the first copy. §8 is the section that decides which of those matters more, and it already has. **What the ruling does NOT buy, and this is the half that must travel with it: a re-crawl produces A corpus, not THE corpus.** The sites move on. Every measurement taken against the lost database is unreproducible in principle and not merely inconvenient — see M1.97 and §10.7. | §10.4b, §10.7, §8 |
| M1.97 | **Fifteen recorded measurements lost their evidence, and nothing in this document says so.** A re-crawl reproduces the corpus's *shape*, not its *values*: `navucko.com`'s newest post is a different post now, `zecplus.de`'s two robots origins may have been reconciled by its operator, and a shop that gained a product sitemap since 17 August leaves §10.3's *3 of 13* silently wrong. Left unmarked, each of these reads as a live number that a later unit may re-check and "correct" — and the correction would be a **different measurement wearing the old one's citation**, which is M1.42's two-expressions defect with a year between the expressions. | **§10.7 is the register, and the marker is `MEASURED <date>, NOT REPRODUCIBLE`** — one greppable string, carried at the site of each measurement and tabulated once. **Not deleted**, because a finding does not become false when its evidence expires — M1.61's zecplus reasoning stands on its own logic and it was the reasoning, not the byte counts, that changed the lookup. **Not restated as if still checkable**, which is the failure this closes. Each entry carries the date the measurement was taken, because the dates differ by up to three days and a single "the lost corpus" date would itself be the scalar this project keeps finding. **The direction of error is deliberate: a marked-unreproducible number that could in fact be re-measured costs a later unit one re-measurement, while an unmarked one that cannot costs it a false correction it will believe.** | §10.7, §10.2, §10.3 |
| M1.98 | **§10.4b said the interrupted M5 work was GONE, the stash is on this machine and complete, and the false statement had reached three shipped migration headers.** `git stash list` returns `stash@{0}: On main: interrupted-M5-remnant`, at base commit **`6a5e266`** — the exact commit §10.4b names — taken 2026-08-17 16:17:17. It was stashed with `-u`, so it has a third parent and **the untracked half survived too**: `portal/pagespeed.py` (8,983 bytes), `portal/verify.py` (5,846) and `portal/migrations/010_phase2_writers.sql` (19,440). Checked item by item against Unit 5's inventory, **all six are present** — `phase2_input_settled` (15 hits in the tracked diff), the `settled` term (23), three-state `_own_brand` (5) and `_owner_operated` (4), plus the three files. **Unit 6 did not get this wrong; the register did.** Unit 6's own findings document states it *conditionally* and correctly — *"The work is not lost **if** the machine Unit 5 ran on still exists"* — and §10.4b hardened that into *"GONE and cannot be recovered"*. **The lost qualifier is the entire defect**, and the condition was true: Unit 5 ran at `/workspaces/lead-Portal` in the codespace created 2026-08-15T01:37:43Z, which is the one this was verified on. From there the unconditional form propagated to **seven sites** across two units, three of them shipped schema — `010_score_evaluated_on.sql` (*"UNRECOVERABLE FILE"*), `011_phase2_view_columns.sql`, `015_batch_requests.sql`, §10.6's `balance_exhausted` row, and both unit findings documents. **Nobody re-ran the one-word command**, in any container, for four units. | **§10.4b, §10.6 and all three migration headers are corrected, and the stash is left exactly where it is** — not popped, not applied. **This is a register correction, not a recovery**: 9a independently rebuilt `verify.py` and the comparison is worth more than the file — same structural ruling (*the verifier takes the sent text and cannot reach an artifact*), different prose, arrived at twice, which is the strongest evidence available that the ruling is right. **What is genuinely still unbuilt is now knowable rather than guessed**: `portal/pagespeed.py` was never rebuilt and `pagespeed` appears nowhere in the tree, and of migration `010`'s five changes only `contact.vat_id` has a writer today, while `run.pagespeed_calls` exists nowhere and `llm_batch.status = 'balance_exhausted'` is still §10.6's *ahead of its writer*. **The class: a negative observation made in one working copy was written into the register as a property of the world.** A stash is local by definition — which is exactly why "it is not here" is evidence about a container and never about the artefact, and why the sentence needed its `if` more than any other sentence in §10.4b. | §10.4b, §10.6, migrations `010`/`011`/`015` |
| M1.99 | **§7 control 9 says *"every API key from environment variables"* and says nothing about a credential that is not an API key.** A triage session authorised to run one read-only `messages.batches.list` found `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` unset, no `ant` CLI, and no credential file — and `~/.claude/.credentials.json` holding a `claudeAiOauth` block with an `accessToken`, sitting in the home directory of the process that had been asked to make the call. It declined to use it and reported no credential. **Control 9 did not tell it to decline**; the control governs where an API key comes from, and this was not an API key. The next session in that position has a brief that wants a listing, a control that is silent, and a working token one `Authorization` header away — and the failure is not hypothetical spend, it is **spend or quota drawn against a human's personal subscription by a process authorised only to read metadata**, with no `run` row, no reservation, and nothing in §7's ledger able to see it. | **§7 control 9 gains the distinction explicitly: a CLI subscription OAuth token is not an API key, and no code path in this project may use one.** Three reasons, and the third is the one that generalises. *(a)* **Different credential, different surface**: the token authenticates a person to their Claude Code session, not this application to the Anthropic API. *(b)* **It is invisible to every control in §7**: controls 2, 3 and 4 reserve and sum against `run.est_cost_usd`, and consumption on a subscription token produces no row anywhere — the ledger would read `$0.00` and be *correct*, which is worse than reading wrong. *(c)* **Authorisation to read is not authorisation to reach for a different credential to read with.** The scope of a permission is the action *and* the means; a session that substitutes the means has exceeded the permission while believing it complied. **Written now, before 9c needs a real key**, because the moment a brief authorises spend the convenient credential is still sitting there and the control that should refuse it will otherwise still be silent. The triage session's refusal was correct and is recorded as the ruling rather than as a fact about one session's judgement. | §7 control 9 |
| M1.100 | **"No batch was submitted" is unverified, and it was about to be recorded as zero.** Every local instrument that could answer is gone or was never written: `llm_batch` went with the database, and no code has ever written to it — `extract-p2` stops before §7 control 4's reservation by design (M1.83). The account-scoped listing that *would* answer has never been run: the triage session was authorised for exactly one read-only `messages.batches.list`, found no credential, and **reported the question open rather than reporting zero.** That was right, and the reason it was right is already ruled in this project: **M1.59's tri-state**, where *unavailable* (we cannot tell) and *unrestricted* (there is nothing) are distinct states with distinct consequences, applied here to our own audit trail instead of to a shop's robots.txt. The evidence against submission is strong but is all of one kind — `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` was unset in every session, CI's `assert-no-api-key` step fails the build if it is present, and the 9c transcript records `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: NOT SET` at 04:34:20 — and *no key on this machine* is a statement about this machine, which is **M1.98's defect exactly**, one day later and in the opposite direction. | **Recorded in §10.7 as OPEN with its closing procedure, not as zero.** Closed by either: `messages.batches.list` on a machine holding a real API key, or the Console's usage view for the billing account. **A batch that exists means submission happened and the spend is committed** whether or not results were ever read, results stay retrievable for **29 days** from creation (§5.6), retrieval costs nothing extra, and **resubmitting would double the cost** — so the listing must precede any 9c work that would submit, and its `provider_batch_id` is the only route back to results now that `llm_batch` is gone. **What makes this an amendment rather than a note: the register is the audit trail, and an audit trail that records unverified conclusions as measurements is the instrument defect M1.91 was about**, one level further out. M1.91 caught citations that pointed at no row; this catches a row that would have pointed at no measurement. | §10.7, §7, §5.6 |

### Amendments from M5 completion — §5.5a, the spend gate, and three rows the build falsified — 2026-09-03

**No crawl, no API call, no key, no spend.** This unit built what §10.6 had been tracking as absent: `portal/pagespeed.py` and its migration `016`, and the production caller for `extract_p2.reserve_and_submit`. It also carried audit findings 8–10 from the 2026-09-02 external audit. **Migrations taken: `016`. Next free: `017`.** Suite: 715 → **760 passed, 2 skipped, 139 subtests**, `env -u ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, ruff clean, politeness job green in both halves.

| # | Question or defect | Resolution | Section |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1.101 | **§5.5a was the last unbuilt stage of M5, and building it falsified three rows of this document in the same commit.** `pagespeed` appeared nowhere in the tree; `run.pagespeed_calls` existed in no migration; `opp.slow_site` (+10) had been declared since M0 and dormant for three milestones because nothing wrote `perf.lighthouse_performance`. **The reader was never the missing half** — `company_profile` has projected that key to `lighthouse_perf` since migration 001, and M3's audit already guarded the rule against a NULL reading as a low score. What was missing was one writer. **M1.98's stash is not the source**: it is on a machine this container cannot reach, so *"it is not here"* remains a statement about a container, and the rebuild is from §5.3's row and §5.5a's sentence rather than from a file nobody can open. | **Built, with three decisions recorded as normative in the module rather than left implicit, because each is a place a later reader would otherwise "fix" toward the wrong answer.** *(a)* **Its own `run.stage = 'pagespeed'`**, though §5.5a says PageSpeed runs *"here"* in `extract-p2`: migration 006 scopes `company_profile` to the latest finished run **per (company, stage)**, and *"a later run of a stage is authoritative for everything that stage owns, including the keys it deliberately did not write"* — so under a shared stage the LLM submission's run and the measurement's run would retract each other's signals per company, whichever finished second. *(b)* **The 30-day cache writes NOTHING for a cached company**, which under that same scoping is what keeps the earlier run's number being served; writing the old value again under a new `observed_at` would be a measurement that was never taken. *(c)* **The cache clock is the measurement's own `signal.observed_at`, not §5.3's literal `artifact.last_checked_at`** — D5(b) advances that column on every crawl, so a weekly `fetch` would make the 30-day rule either never or always re-measure. The literal reading is one predicate in `_CACHED_SQL` if it was meant. **`016` ships with its writer** (M1.45(c)); it counts requests **issued**, because a quota is consumed by the request and not by its success, and it touches no `est_cost_usd` — §7.1 prices this API at $0.000 and a count of free calls must not enter a ledger that sums money. **No keyless branch exists**: the API accepts unkeyed requests against a shared quota, and a call §7 control 1's Console cap cannot see is exactly what control 9's last sentence refuses. | §10.6, §5.5a, §5.3, migration `016` |
| M1.102 | **The spend gate was the wrong shape: a refusal nobody can pass is not a gate.** 9a made `extract-p2` exit 2 without `--dry-run` because the submitting path needed §7 control 4's reservation and that was 9b's. **9b built it** — `reserve_and_submit`, tested against a fake provider — and the refusal stayed, with a message that still named the reservation as 9b's work. Two defects, and the second is the one that matters. The message was **stale**, which is M1.98's class in miniature. But the gate's *shape* meant the first real spend would have been made **by editing the function**, which is the one way a gate must never be passed: the edit is invisible to review as a decision, carries no record of who authorised it, and leaves nothing for §7 to point at afterwards. | **Restated, not removed.** The safe default is unchanged in substance and different in form: **with no flag the command is a dry run** — what would be sent, who is withheld and why, no request, no key, no reservation — and **`--submit` is the written authorisation, expressed at the command line**, which is where the spend is made and therefore where the record belongs. `--submit --dry-run` is refused as a contradiction rather than resolved either way. The paid path keeps control 4's order exactly: §7 control 2's ceiling **first**, so a window already over budget never asks the provider to count a token; then the reservation writing `llm_batch.est_cost_usd` **and** `run.est_cost_usd` in one transaction (M1.72); then `submit`. The submitting run is **finished on success** because `reconcile` writes this batch's signals under it (B4) and `company_profile` serves a stage only from a finished, un-aborted run (007) — an open run would have made every Phase-2 signal invisible. `MissingKeyError` (before the reservation) and `BalanceExhausted` (after it, money counted, batch `reserved`) each mark the run aborted and exit 2. **§10.7b is untouched by this**: the listing that establishes whether money has ever been spent still precedes any real submission. | §7 controls 2–4, §5.5b, §10.7b |
| M1.103 | **Three audit findings were fixed whose common shape is that a bound was written where it could not bind.** *(8)* `_json_payload` raised `LLMConfigError` on a `succeeded` result whose message was truncated at `max_tokens`, refused, or otherwise not JSON — and one such company aborted the whole poll, so every good extraction in the batch went unwritten. *(9)* `MAX_BODY_BYTES` was applied as `response.content[:max_bytes]`, which caps the **slice** and not the **read**: httpx had already inflated the entire `Content-Encoding: gzip` body into memory first. *(10)* `cmd_fetch` built its targets from the seed file and never consulted `company.excluded`, so §6.4 duplicates and `robots_disallowed` rows were re-crawled on every run — against a host already crawled under its own row, and against a host that had said no. | **Each becomes a bound that binds.** *(8)* A disposition on that one request, carrying its reason to `llm_batch_request` and — because the response **was paid for** — **carrying its usage**, so `actual_cost_usd` counts it; reading only `extraction.usage` would have released spent money back into the ledger, the one direction §7 must not fail in. Not resubmittable: the same request at the same bound truncates again. A truncated prefix is **not parsed even when it would happen to parse**. *(9)* `Fetcher.get` streams and `_read_capped` stops consuming at the cap, closing the response behind it; redirect bodies are never read at all. **Measured against the old code, because a cap that cannot be observed is not a cap**: on a 64 MiB-of-zeros bomb served in 16 raw chunks, the old path drained all 16 and held **67,108,864 bytes decoded in memory** before slicing to 1 MiB, where the streamed path takes 1. The residual bound — the cap plus one decoded raw read — is written into the docstring rather than left to be discovered. *(10)* The same `excluded = 0` predicate `extract-p1` has always applied, with the skip **printed**: lifting an exclusion is an operator's act, not a stage's. | §5.6, §5.2, §6.4, §7 control 12 |

### Amendments from Unit 10 — completing the build without the first spend — 2026-09-03

**No crawl, no API call, no key, no spend.** Unit 10 builds everything the brief's build order still lists that can be built and tested without a credential: §5.7's warning that was specified and never read, §10.7b's closing procedure as a command, M6 and M8 behind M1.102's gate, and M7 whole. **After this unit, every milestone M0–M8 has code, and the only things left undone need a credential: §10.7b's listing, and then 9c.** **Migrations taken: `017`. Next free: `018`.** Written from a container that holds no key and never reached a network; every paid path here was exercised against a fake provider under `env -u ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.

| # | Question or defect | Resolution | Section |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1.104 | **§5.7 said `score --phase 2` "warns loudly if unreconciled batches exist for the companies being scored", and nothing read the sentence; and §10.7b's closing procedure was a pasted one-liner, which is M1.73's shape.** `cmd_score` accepted `--phase 2` since M3 and wrote a `phase=2` row over whatever `company_profile` held, with no word about a batch in flight for those companies — a score a reader copies into a brief before `reconcile` moves it. Separately, M1.100 ruled *whether a batch was ever submitted* OPEN and gave a Python snippet to close it; a procedure that has to be typed is reported on without being run. | **Both become things that run.** `score.unreconciled_batches` answers on `reconciled_at IS NULL` and nothing else — §7 control 12(b) makes that the one column that says *the measured actual was written*, so a `reserved` batch with no provider id is unreconciled, correctly. The CLI prints every such batch with its companies to stderr **before** the score is written, and still writes it: the number is the best reading available, and `reconcile` will supersede it under the submitting run's own id (B4). Phase 1 never warns; it reads no Phase-2 signal. `portal llm-batches` is `messages.batches.list` classified **free** in the import-time guard (M1.71): no key is exit 2 and *"§10.7b stays OPEN, not zero"*, an empty listing is *closed at zero*, a non-empty one prints every id with the resubmission warning. It looks for no other credential (§7 control 9). | §5.7, §10.7b, §7 control 12 |
| M1.105 | **M6 was unblocked (M1.54) and not started; building it needed three decisions §5.5c does not take, and one number §7 cannot measure.** *(a)* §5.5c derives queries *"from `one_line_offer` and `product_categories`"* but the offer line is prose, and a query minted from prose by a heuristic is one nobody can reproduce — which defeats recording `ai.query_text` at all. *(b)* M1.52 forbids a heuristic in the reservation because `count_tokens` is free; here the search results are not known until the search runs, and on Haiku 4.5 they land in context in full (M1.54). *(c)* M1.102 aborts `extract-p2` on a dry balance because nothing was written; here every company's six keys are committed before the next company's first call, and an aborted run is one `company_profile` refuses to serve (007) — which would discard signals real money bought. | **`portal ai-check`, dry by default, paid on `--submit`, its own `run.stage = 'ai_check'` for M1.101(a)'s reason.** *(a)* **The first product category is the term and the templates are fixed** (`beste {term}`, `{term} Test`, `{term} kaufen`); a company with no category is **withheld, not guessed for**, and the dry run says whether that is because the homepage extraction never ran or because it ran and returned none. `ai.query_text` is therefore reproducible from the profile alone. Fewer than two queries is refused at the CLI, not configured away (M1.23). *(b)* **`count_tokens` on the prompt plus `SEARCH_CONTEXT_TOKENS = 20,000` per query** — the top of §5.5c's own 10–20k range, dated `2026-08-15`, the one unmeasured number in the reservation, and it **errs toward over-reservation**, which is control 3's stated preference. The measured `usage` replaces it when the response arrives — seconds, not a batch's 24 hours. `max_uses = 1` is sent to the provider so the searches priced are the searches possible (M1.103's rule). Control 3's per-run ceiling is `$5.00`, checked before any run row exists. *(c)* **A dry balance finishes the run** with `companies_seen` at what was reached, prints the shortfall by name, reconciles to the measured actual, and exits 2. Nothing is claimed for the companies not reached. **Migration `017`** projects the four basis keys §8's brief must refuse without, and `offer.product_categories` — which 011 left out under A2 §4's *"nothing scores them"*, a ruling about readers, and this is now the reader. `impressum.legal_name` stays on `company` where A2 §4 put it; the brand match reads it there. `opp.ai_invisible`'s settledness predicate is no longer `_never_settled`: a written `ai.queries_checked` is the fact of having looked. | §5.5c, §7 controls 3 and 8, §10.6, migration `017`, A2 §4 |
| M1.106 | **M7 was four commands and three §9 actions that nothing had built, and one of them — `forget` — makes a claim that must be measured rather than asserted.** The brief's definition of done says *"`forget --domain X` leaves zero rows anywhere"*; §4 put `ON DELETE CASCADE` on every `company_id` and migration 015 denormalised one out of a text key for exactly this — but a **file does not cascade**, `score_component` hangs off `score` and not off `company`, and `foreign_keys` is a per-connection pragma that CASCADE silently ignores when off. §8's brief rule is the other measured claim: *"an export missing any basis field must fail, not degrade gracefully."* | **`portal purge`, `forget`, `exclude`, `outreach`, `brief`, and the same three writes on the §9 page.** `forget` deletes through the schema and then **measures the residue**: every table with a `company_id` (read from `sqlite_master`, not listed, so a later table cannot be missed), `score_component` through its parent, and the domain's directory under the artifact root; it asserts `PRAGMA foreign_keys = 1` before deleting rather than trusting it; it exits 1 and says *"do not report this as done"* if anything remains. **Two things it keeps, deliberately: `run` rows, because a deletion must never release spend (§7 control 12c), and `llm_batch`, which names no company.** `--yes` is required. `purge` prints each expired contact and deletes in one statement. `exclude` refuses an empty reason (§4: *never exclude silently*) and `--lift` is the operator's act M1.103(10) keeps out of every stage. `outreach` inserts the row migration 008's trigger guards and turns the trigger's refusal into the flags that caused it; `email` is refused at the argument (§8). **`brief.render` raises `MissingBasis` where an `ai.*` result exists without all three basis fields, omits the section where no check ran, and raises `ContactBlocked` for a company whose score is knowingly too high (A7's third axis) — a brief is the document the refused `outreach` row would accompany.** The result line is the count and never a general claim; the competitor line renders only from `ai.competitors_mentioned`; abstentions are listed as *nicht bewertbar* rather than dropped. On the page each action posts to its own endpoint behind Audit 5's cross-site check; a §8 refusal comes back as its reason with 409. | §8, §9, §7 control 12, migration 008, migration 015 |
| M1.107 | **M8 was the last row of the build order, deliberately, and building it exposed that `fetch` could not reach what it wrote.** §5.1 says *field mask `displayName`, `websiteUri`, `formattedAddress` only* and the brief's definition of done says the mask *is exactly* that — a claim about bytes on the wire, not about a constant. `run.places_calls` had been in §4 since M0 with no writer. And `cmd_fetch` took `--seed` as required and built its targets from the file: a company `discover` wrote would never have been crawled. | **`portal discover`, dry by default, keyed only on `--submit`; `--seed` optional on `fetch`.** The mask is a module constant sent as `X-Goog-FieldMask` and **asserted on the request httpx actually sends** (`httpx.MockTransport`), with `rating` asserted absent — asking for it moves the call to a dearer SKU (§5.1). The key rides in a header and never in a URL; a failure re-raises as the exception's class name and nothing of its text, `pagespeed`'s habit (M1.101). `run.places_calls` counts requests **issued**, before the outcome is known, for M1.101's reason, and touches no `est_cost_usd`: **§7 control 1 is the Console cap, and no code can confirm it, so the dry run says the sentence instead of ticking a box.** Pagination follows `nextPageToken` only while `MAX_CALLS_PER_RUN` allows and reports `capped` rather than reading on. Dedupe is `seeds.normalise_domain` — the seed loader's single expression — plus `company.domain`'s UNIQUE with `DO NOTHING`, so an overlapping re-run inserts nothing twice; a place with no `websiteUri` is a shop this tool cannot fetch and is counted, not raised. **`fetch` without `--seed` fetches every company row** — the same set `extract-p1` and `score` read — and with one behaves as before. | §5.1, §4, §7 control 1, §5.2 |

The finishing was right (the paid signals stand, M1.105(c)); the silence was M1.39's shape — a run whose end has to be inferred from `companies_seen`. *(2)* `list_batches` iterated the SDK's page object, which auto-fetches the next page, so `--limit` was a page size and the printed *"limit N"* was a claim the code did not keep. *(3)* `SEARCHES_PER_QUERY`, read by the CLI, absent from `__all__`. | **Each bound now binds, and each has the test that would have caught it.** *(1)* Every exception between two companies is the balance case for everything that matters: the run is finished with what was reached, `Report.stopped_by` carries the class and text, the CLI prints it by name with the companies not reached and exits 2 — no traceback. `run.aborted_reason` was considered and rejected: it would hide the paid signals (007), which is the whole of (c). The test fails the provider with a `RuntimeError` after company A and asserts A is served, B is absent, and the reason is on stderr. *(2)* `itertools.islice` bounds the iteration; the fake page object in the test auto-iterates seven rows and `limit=3` returns three. *(3)* Listed. **What the audit did not find, stated because absence is a finding too:** no `evidence_url` written by M6 or M8 is a page, and `ai-check:<model>:<date>` renders on the §9 page as a dead link under `ai_queries_checked`'s evidence — a cosmetic gap, recorded here rather than fixed, because the honest value is a non-URL and a fake URL would be worse. | §5.5c, §10.7b, M1.39 |

### Amendments from Unit 11 — PR #7 reconciled onto `main` — 2026-09-04

**Unit 9c's work, landed two weeks late and renumbered to land at all.** PR #7 measured the rebuilt corpus, met two real servers, built §7 control 3 and found the defect in §10.7b's own closing procedure — then never merged, while PR #8 and PR #9 took its amendment numbers from the same `main`. M1.113 is that collision and its resolution; M1.109–M1.112 are #7's rows, renumbered and carried over. **The rows below are 21 August's findings; only their numbers changed, and each says where it was superseded.** **Migrations taken: none. Next free: `018`.** Nothing here was run against a network and no batch was submitted; `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is not read by any path this unit added.

| # | Question or defect | Resolution | Section |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1.109 | **§7 control 3 has been specified since v0.3 and was never implemented, and §7 says it is the control that should bite.** Control 2 is explicitly the *outer bound* — a runaway guard whose own text says it is not a budget and whose expected steady state is $31–36 against $45. Control 3 is the one meant to fire in normal operation, and `ledger.MONTHLY_CEILING_USD = 45.0` was **the only ceiling constant in the tree**; `grep` for a per-run one returned nothing. **Reproduced before it was built**, on `95d3281`, through the real reservation write: one run charged $2.00 six times reached `run.est_cost_usd = $12.00` — past a stated per-run ceiling of $5.00 on the third call — and `check_ceiling` then **cleared it**, correctly, reporting $12.00 of $45.00 with $33.00 of headroom. Nothing objected, because nothing was there to object. Control 2's own text names the gap it leaves: *"`run.est_cost_usd` resets on every invocation, so ten aborted-and-retried runs cost ten times the per-run limit"* — which is an argument for why control 2 is needed **as well**, and had been read for two units as though it made control 3 optional. | **`ledger.RUN_CEILING_USD = 5.0`, `RunCeilingExceeded`, and `charge_run`, which takes a `LedgerClearance`.** Four choices, each for a direction of error. *(a)* **The clearance is required, so control 3 composes with control 2 rather than replacing it.** A clearance is unforgeable — `check_ceiling` is the only thing that builds one — so the per-run ceiling cannot be applied *instead of* the outer bound by a caller who preferred the smaller number. *(b)* **`RunCeilingExceeded` is deliberately not a subclass of `CeilingExceeded`.** Control 2 firing means something is wrong; control 3 firing is ordinary. A shared type lets an `except CeilingExceeded` written for the runaway case swallow the routine one, which is the direction that spends money. *(c)* **The check is on the post-charge total, not the increment**, so a single reservation larger than the whole ceiling is refused outright — otherwise the guard's first call is free, and the first call is the one most likely to be pathological. *(d)* **It is enforced at the single write**, inside `_commit_reservation`'s `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, so a refusal rolls the batch row back with it: **no batch on the books, no money counted, nothing submitted** — and a future second caller gets the ceiling by construction rather than by remembering. **Ported to `main` by Unit 11 with one change of substance: `ai_visibility.PER_RUN_CEILING_USD = 5.00` — a second `$5.00` and a second `RunCeilingExceeded`, written by M1.105 while this branch sat open — is deleted, and M6 now reads `ledger.RUN_CEILING_USD` and raises `ledger.RunCeilingExceeded`.** One control, one constant, one exception class, **two enforcement points**, and the two are not redundant: M6 can price a whole run before the `run` row exists and refuses there, reserving nothing; M5's batch price is known only per call, so it refuses at the write. Two constants for one policy bound is M1.42's shape applied to a policy instead of to a corpus, and the direction of the resulting error is a ceiling raised in one place and not the other. | §7 control 3 |
| M1.110 | **A per-run ceiling that can refuse a reconciliation would degrade the ledger it exists to protect, and nothing said so.** `run.est_cost_usd` has two writers: the reservation (`extract_p2`, pre-call) and the estimate-to-actual correction (`reconcile`, post-hoc, B3.1/M1.90). Control 3's wording — *"before every LLM call"* — covers the first. Applied to the second it inverts: **the money is already spent**, and refusing the correction because the corrected total lands above $5.00 leaves `run.est_cost_usd` holding a number known to be wrong. Control 2 sums that column, so a per-run guard blocking its own bookkeeping makes the guard that *actually* bounds spend read a falsehood — and it fails in the **under-counting** direction, the one §7 must not fail in, which is M1.69's whole argument arriving through a different door. | **`ledger.reconcile_run` exists solely so the omission is a ruling rather than an accident.** `reconcile` previously wrote the column with its own inline `UPDATE`, so control 3 not applying to it would have been a property of one call site nobody had stated. It is now a named function whose docstring carries the rule, and a test drives an actual that takes a run to $7.90 — well past the $5.00 ceiling — and asserts it lands. **Separate from `charge_run` rather than a flag on it**, for the reason `_charge_run` is separate from `_write_batch_row`: *reserve* and *reconcile* are different operations with different rules, and one function taking a boolean is one place for a later edit to conflate them. The delta may be negative, and the ordinary case is an over-estimate correcting downward. | §7 control 3, §5.6 |
| M1.111 | **`contact.purge_after` is `NOT NULL` and names a command that has never existed.** The column's own comment reads *"collected_at + 12 months; enforced by `portal purge` (§8)"*. `git grep` for `purge` or `forget` in `portal/` returns nothing, and the CLI's subcommand list does not contain either. **Every contact row this project ever writes will carry a deletion deadline that nothing reads.** This is M1.45(c)'s shape — a documented resolution path with no writer — sitting in the schema rather than in a review-reason table, and it reads to anyone inspecting the schema as though erasure is handled. It became material in this unit rather than in M7: `extract-p2 --dry-run` prepared **nine** Impressum pages for extraction, and Phase 2's whole output is `full_name`, `role`, `email`, `phone` and `postal_address` for natural persons. **The unit came within one API key of writing nine contact rows into a table whose erasure command does not exist.** | **Recorded with the manual procedure written out, and not built — M7 is a milestone and this unit's scope is the first spend.** What is added is the part that was never stated: **`ON DELETE CASCADE` appears nine times in the schema and every one hangs off `company(id)`, so deleting a contact cascades to nothing**, and the subject's name also lives in `signal.value_text`, in `signal.evidence_url` and `contact.source_url` (an Impressum URL is frequently identifying for a sole trader), in the stored artifact body under `data/artifacts/`, and — for 29 days after a submission, unreachably — at the provider (§5.6). `data/requests.jsonl` is append-only **by design** and holds every URL fetched. So honouring an erasure request today is a hand-written multi-table `DELETE`, a file deletion, an edit to a log deliberately made append-only, and a window nothing here can close. **Recorded now rather than at M7 because the first `contact` row is what makes it real**, and because a schema that claims an erasure path it does not have is worse than one that claims nothing. | §8, §4, §10.6 |
| M1.112 | **§10.7b's own closing procedure could not tell *there is no batch* from *the question was never asked*, and the ambiguous branch is the one that authorises spend.** Run exactly as §10.7b wrote it, on 2026-08-21 at 21:39 UTC: **exit status 1, zero bytes on `stdout`**, and a `TypeError` on `stderr` from `_validate_headers` — raised during *client construction*, so **no network call was made at all**. The procedure's stated reading is *"if it prints nothing, no batch exists"*; a caller reading `stdout`, or a wrapper that discards `stderr`, would have closed a money question that was never put. **The consequence is not a bookkeeping error.** The decision this listing gates is whether to submit: a batch that exists is committed spend, results stay retrievable for 29 days at no extra cost, and **resubmitting doubles it** (§5.6, M1.100) — so the silent branch fails in the direction that spends money twice. **M1.100 wrote the ruling and then wrote a procedure that manufactures the very zero the ruling forbids** — the register row says *not zero, and it may not be recorded as zero*, and its own instrument produced an unmarked zero on its first execution. **The class is M1.59's tri-state turned on our own audit trail**: *unavailable* and *empty* are distinct states, and this procedure collapsed them. It is also **M1.98's defect arriving through the instrument rather than through the prose** — a fact about one machine's credentials presenting itself as a fact about the account. **A second instance of the same class landed in the same five minutes**: this unit was told `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` *"is now set as a Codespaces secret"* and it was **absent from the environment of every process that would use it** — not in the shell, not in `claude`'s environ, not in **PID 1's**, in a container whose `docker-init` had started at 21:36:39 UTC, three minutes before the check. *Set in a configuration store* and *present at the point of use* are different statements, and the unit was handed the first as though it were the second. | **The listing must end by printing a terminal verdict line, and that line — not the absence of rows — is the evidence.** §10.7b now specifies `print(f'LISTING COMPLETED — {n} batch(es) on this account.')` after the loop, with the client constructed on its own statement so a missing credential raises visibly rather than inside a comprehension. **If `LISTING COMPLETED` is not printed, for any reason, §10.7b stays open** — including the reason observed here, a traceback on a stream the caller was not reading. The exit status is named as a second check, and the Console route gains the same rule: *a view that did not render is not a view showing nothing.* **Direction of error, and it is the whole point: the hardened procedure can only fail toward *still open*.** A run that wrongly leaves the question open costs one re-run of a free, read-only call. A run that wrongly closes it authorises a resubmission against a batch that is already paid for. Those are not comparable costs, so the instrument is built to fail toward the cheap one. **The general rule, which is the half that outlives this command: a negative result must be a printed statement, never an absence of output** — an instrument that reports *nothing* by saying nothing cannot be distinguished from an instrument that did not run. **And a credential is verified in the environment of the process that will make the call**, never in the store that is supposed to supply it; §7 control 9 says keys come from the environment, and this adds that *the environment is also where their presence is measured*. **Neither half required a key to find**, which is why both are recorded by a unit that never got one. **Unit 11 records that the instrument half of this was built independently while PR #7 sat open: `portal llm-batches` (M1.104) prints a terminal statement on all three branches — no key is exit 2 and *"§10.7b stays OPEN, not zero"*, an empty listing is *closed at zero* with the date, a non-empty one prints every id — which is exactly what this row asked for, reached without reading it.** Two lines arriving at the same instrument from the same defect is evidence about the defect, not a coincidence. What was **not** done is the prose: §10.7b still carried the original pasted snippet, the one that produced the unmarked zero, and a procedure a reader copies is the procedure that runs. §10.7b now names the command first and keeps the verdict-line rule for the snippet and the Console route both. | §10.7b, §7 control 9, §5.6 |
| M1.113 | **Two units numbered themselves M1.101–M1.105 from the same register at the same time, and both were right to.** PR #7 (`claude/unit9c-first-spend`, 21 August, 7 commits) took **M1.101–M1.105** and stayed open, unmerged, for two weeks. PR #8 (M5 completion) then branched from the same `main` — `95d3281`, where the register ended at M1.100 — and took **M1.101–M1.103**; PR #9 (Unit 10) branched from #8 and took **M1.104–M1.108**. Neither line could see the other's numbers, because **the register is a file on a branch and "next free" is a property of the branch you are standing on**, not of the project. M1.91's check cannot catch this: it verifies that every cited number *resolves to a row*, and after both lines merge every number still resolves — to **the wrong row**, silently, which is worse than the dangling citation M1.91 was built to refuse. Merged naively the tree would have carried `M1.101` meaning *§7 control 3 was built* in one document and *`max_uses` is sent so the bound binds* in another, and a reader would have had no way to tell which. | **PR #7's rows are renumbered onto the end of the register and #7 is closed in favour of this branch: M1.101→M1.109, M1.102→M1.110, M1.104→M1.111, M1.105→M1.112**, substituted at every citation in the spec, the ported code and `docs/unit9c-first-spend-findings.md`. **#7's M1.103 is not carried as a row.** It recorded that M1.75's robots-collapse *"over-reports"* has a measured recurring price and resolved to *"nothing is changed"*; it is an update to M1.75, not a decision of its own, so its content stays in the findings and its four citations point at M1.75. Renumbering the *later* line was rejected: #8 and #9 are merged, green, and cite each other across nine files, so moving them would edit merged history to accommodate a branch that never landed. **What was ported:** §7 control 3 whole — `RUN_CEILING_USD`, `RunCeilingExceeded`, `charge_run`, `run_reserved_usd`, `reconcile_run`, enforcement at `_charge_run` inside M1.72's transaction, and the CLI wiring `extract-p2 --submit` never had. **What was superseded and dropped:** #7's hardened §10.7b snippet, because `portal llm-batches` (M1.104) is the same instrument built better (M1.112). **The general rule: a branch that holds register numbers is holding a lock on a shared resource, and an unmerged branch holds it indefinitely.** Coining a number is cheap; the cost lands on whoever merges second, and it lands as ambiguity rather than as a conflict, because git merges two disjoint table rows without a word. Long-lived branches take their numbers **at merge**, or they take a reserved block and say so in the register on `main` when they open. | §10.7b, §7 control 3, M1.91 |

### Amendment from the §10.7b closing — 2026-09-04

**One row, one measurement, no code.** `portal llm-batches` was run against a real key for the billing account and answered the question M1.100 ruled open four units ago. **Migrations taken: none. Next free: `018`.** No paid call was made — the listing is classified free in the import-time guard (M1.71) — and no batch was submitted.

| # | Question or defect | Resolution | Section |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1.114 | **§10.7b was open for four units, and it is closed by one free read-only call: the account holds no message batches.** M1.100 ruled the question OPEN rather than zero because every local instrument that could answer was gone or had never been written — `llm_batch` went with the destroyed database and nothing had ever written a row to it — and the account-scoped listing that *would* answer had never been run. Four sessions across three units reported *no key on this machine* and correctly declined to read that as *no batch on this account* (M1.98, M1.59's tri-state). M1.112 then found that §10.7b's own procedure, run as written, printed **zero bytes and exited 1**, so the one attempt to close it would have closed it silently and wrongly. | **CLOSED on 2026-09-04 with the answer ZERO, by measurement.** `portal llm-batches` (M1.104) was run against a real key for the billing account: it listed no batches and printed its terminal verdict line — *"§10.7b is CLOSED with the answer ZERO, as of 2026-09-04T00:39:17Z: no batch has ever been submitted on this key's account"* — at **exit 0**. **The verdict line is the evidence, not the absence of rows**, which is exactly M1.112's rule doing the work it was written for, on its first real use. **9c is unblocked**: there is no committed spend to account for, no `provider_batch_id` to recover, and nothing a first `--submit` would double. **What is recorded, and it is the half that generalises: the measurement agreed with four units of inference, and the agreement is not what closed the question.** The listing closed it. Had the two disagreed, every fact those units recorded would still have been true and the answer would still have been the listing's — which is why *strong evidence, all of one kind* was never allowed to become the value. §10.7b's status line moves from OPEN to CLOSED; its closing procedure stays in the spec, because the instrument outlives the answer. | §10.7b, §7 control 9, §5.6 |

### Amendments from the first `--submit` — 2026-09-04

**The first real submission was refused by the provider with a 400, and no batch was created.** The defect it exposed had shipped in Unit 9b and had been asserted by three tests ever since. **Migrations taken: none. Next free: `018`.** The refused reservation is still on the books and is not released by this amendment — see §7 control 4 and migration 014.

| # | Question or defect | Resolution | Section |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1.115 | **`custom_id` was built with `:` separators, which the batch API does not admit, so every request the pipeline has ever built was unsendable — and the whole batch is refused on one bad key, after the reservation is committed.** `build_requests` had emitted `f"{kind}:{company_id}:{artifact_id}"` since Unit 9b. The batch API constrains `custom_id` to `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,64}$`; a colon is not in it. **This was invisible to the suite by construction, and that is the finding rather than the typo.** Every fake provider's `submit_batch` accepted any string, so nothing in 829 tests modelled the one constraint that mattered, and three tests — `test_extract_p2.py`, two in `test_extract_p2_cli.py` — *asserted the colon form*, pinning the defect as intended behaviour. A fake that accepts what the real surface refuses does not merely fail to catch a defect; it certifies it. The failure mode is the worst available shape: **the 400 arrives from `messages.batches.create`, which is called AFTER §7 control 4 has committed the reservation** (migration 014's ordering, deliberately), and it names `requests.0` rather than a company, so one bad key sends nothing and reports an index. A second copy of the format string in `_commit_reservation` — hand-written, not shared — was the same *one expression, two copies* shape as M1.109's frozen default, and would have been caught only at reconciliation, by the cross-check at `reconcile._apply_extraction`. | **Separator changed `:` → `-`; the pattern is stated once in `llm.CUSTOM_ID_PATTERN` and enforced in `BatchRequest.__post_init__`.** The guard is on the frozen dataclass every request passes through, not in `extract_p2`, because a check the fakes can go around is the thing that failed here: no caller — production, fake or test — can now construct a request the batch API would refuse. `extract_p2.format_custom_id` is the single builder, used by both `build_requests` and `_commit_reservation`, and raises rather than truncating, so a bad key fails where it names the company. `parse_custom_id` is strict on the new separator rather than tolerant of both: `portal llm-batches` reported **zero batches on the account both before and after the refused submit** (M1.114), so there is no in-flight key to stay compatible with, and accepting both forms would be a second meaning for one word. Six regression tests, including one that reads the module source to assert the format string has exactly one expression, and one that proves the pre-M1.115 form no longer parses. The three tests that pinned the colon form now assert the pattern instead. | §5.5b, §7 control 4, migration 014 |
| M1.116 | **A reservation of $0.0639135 is on the books for a batch that provably does not exist, and nothing in the design releases it.** Migration 014 defines `status = 'reserved'` as *"we do not know whether this batch was submitted"* and reads it as *the money is gone*, deliberately over-counting, because a reservation released by a rule is how real spend leaves the ledger. That reading is correct for the crash it was written for — a process that dies between `create` and the row write cannot know what happened. **It is not the situation here, and the difference is measurable.** The 400 was a request-validation refusal: no batch id was ever assigned, and `portal llm-batches` — the account-scoped instrument that closed §10.7b — reports zero batches on the account *after* the refused submit. So the outcome is not unknown; it is known to be *nothing was submitted*. | **The row is left exactly as it is, and this amendment records why rather than clearing it.** Nothing automatic releases a reservation, and that rule is not suspended because this instance is provably safe to release — the rule's value is that it holds when the evidence is thin, and an exception written the first time the evidence is good is not a rule. **What is added is the evidence, not the release**: batch 1 is `reserved`, `provider_batch_id IS NULL`, $0.0639135 against run 5, and the account listing at 2026-09-04T12:07:51Z shows nothing. Releasing it is an operator's act. **Until it is released, §7 control 2 reads $0.06 of $45.00 and a re-run of `--submit` reserves a second time**, so the two reservations would stand for one batch's work — the over-count the design prefers to the alternative, and the reason the release is worth doing before the retry rather than after. | §7 control 4, migration 014, §7 control 2 |


### 10.6 Status of each surface — live, dormant-by-design, or ahead of its writer

**Written because the same ambiguity has now been re-discovered three times under three names (M1.57).** A schema object with no writer and a rule that always declines both read as dead code, and both are deliberate here — the spec commits to the whole pipeline and the milestones land it in order. What was missing is a place to look that up.

**Schema objects** (§4), by whether anything writes them today:

| Object | Status | Decided by |
|---|---|---|
| `company`, `artifact`, `signal`, `run`, `sitemap`, `review_flag`, `score`, `score_component`, `company_profile` | **live** | `fetch`, `extract`, `score` |
| `contact_blocking_reason`, `company.contact_blocked` | **live** | migration 008, `score`, `serve` |
| `llm_batch` | **live as of M1.102** — `extract-p2 --submit` writes `reserved` then `submitted`; `reconcile` writes every terminal status. No row exists until a real submission, which is §10.7b's precondition | §5.5b, §5.6 |
| `contact` | **ahead of its writer** — `reconcile` writes it from verified Impressum names (§5.5b), so the first rows arrive with the first reconciled batch. Its deleter exists first (M1.106): `portal purge` reads `purge_after` and `portal forget` cascades through it | §5.5b, §8 |
| `outreach` | **live as of M1.106** — `portal outreach` and the §9 page's form; migration 008's trigger is still the gate | §9, §8 |
| `llm_batch.status = 'balance_exhausted'` | **not in the schema yet** — still true, but the migration number in this row was stale: `010` went to `score.evaluated_on` (M1.74) and the stash's `010_phase2_writers.sql` **survives but cannot reclaim the number** (M1.98). It ships with **9b's** reservation caller, per M1.45(c), on `014` or later | §7 control 11 |
| review reasons `own_brand_undetermined`, `owner_named_undetermined` | **LIVE as of migration 013** — added with their writer in the same commit (M1.81), which is what M1.45(c) requires. `own_brand_undetermined` is raised by `qual.own_brand`, `owner_named_undetermined` by `qual.owner_operated`; neither blocks contact, because both abstentions err too low | M1.49, A7a items 10–11 |
| `artifact.error` values `address_refused:` / `address_unverifiable:` | **live** — written by `net.Fetcher` through `fetch`'s existing recorder; no schema change, the column already holds free-text fetch failures | M1.68 |
| §9's red rendering of `confidence = 0` | **live, and conditional in a way the row it implements did not state (M1.85)** — the red evidence renders beneath a `score_component`, so a rejected value is visible only where its rule still produces one. M1.81's three-state predicates supply that component for every scored §5.5b key. **Not rendered anywhere: a rejected value on a key no §6 rule reads** (the §8/§9-only Impressum fields, `agency.footer_credit_llm`). Recorded, not closed | M1.85, §9 |
| `run.est_cost_usd` as the §7 control 2 ledger | **live as of M1.102** — `extract-p2 --submit` is the writer, reserving into this column and `llm_batch.est_cost_usd` in one transaction (M1.72), and `reconcile` corrects it to the measured actual. It still reads `$0.00` until a batch is actually submitted, which is §10.7b's precondition. The reader shipping first was the point (M1.69–M1.72), and it shipped four units early | §7 controls 2–4 |
| `run.pagespeed_calls` | **live as of migration `016`** — added with its writer in the same commit (M1.45(c), M1.101). Counts PageSpeed requests **issued**, not billed: §7.1 prices the API at $0.000 and this bounds quota, not money | §5.5a, M1.101 |

**Rules** (§6). *Dormant* here means the rule is correct, declared, and cannot fire until Phase 2 supplies its input — which is exactly what `Rule.phase2_reachable` records, so this column is **derived from `ruleset.RULES` and checked by a test** rather than maintained beside it:

| Rule | Status |
|---|---|
| `qual.own_brand` (+10) | **no longer dormant, and no longer exempt (M1.76, M1.81).** A2 gave it a key (`brand.own_brand`) and migration 011 gave it a view column, so `reads=("own_brand", "homepage_extracted")` and `assert_declared`'s named exemption is **deleted** — the exemption existed only because the key did not. Its predicate is three-state: it declines while `llm.homepage_extracted` is absent, fires or declines on a written boolean, and abstains where the extraction ran and could not tell. It still awards nothing until `extract-p2` writes, which is 9b. |
| `qual.owner_operated` (+15) | **partly dormant.** Disjuncts 1 and 2 fire in Phase 1 from `legal_form` and `gf_count`; disjunct 3 (`site.owner_named`) is Phase 2. |
| `opp.ai_invisible` (+15) | **no longer dormant (M1.105).** M6 is built: `portal ai-check --submit` writes `ai.queries_checked` and `ai.brand_mentions`, and the rule fires on `>= 2` and `= 0`. Nothing about the rule, its weight or its predicate changed. It still awards nothing until a check has actually been submitted, which is §10.7b's precondition and 9c's order — the listing precedes the first live call as it precedes the first batch. |
| `opp.slow_site` (+10) | **no longer dormant (M1.101).** §5.5a is built: `portal pagespeed` writes `perf.lighthouse_performance` and the rule fires below 50 on it. The reader was never missing — `company_profile` has projected the key since migration 001 — so nothing about the rule, its weight or its predicate changed. |
| `neg.has_agency` (−20) | **dormant until M5** for the LLM-read `agency_credit`; the deterministic footer credit already fires in Phase 1. |
| every other rule | **live in Phase 1.** |

## Changelog v0.1 → v0.2 (retained for the record)

1. **ScrapeGraphAI removed.** Both extractions use the Anthropic SDK directly with tool-use structured output. (§5.5)
2. **Scoring restructured.** Blog rules became a mutually exclusive ladder; schema rules conditional; the 45-point cap removed. (§6.2)
3. **Two-phase pipeline.** Phase 1 = free deterministic signals for everyone; Phase 2 = paid signals for advancing companies. (§5)
4. **New signal: AI/GEO visibility.** (§5.5c, §6.2)
5. **New signal: review presence** as a free product-strength proxy. (§5.3)
6. **Blog date detection fixed.** Sitemap `<lastmod>` demoted to a hint. (§5.3)
7. **Exclusions softened.** Two-tier exclusion, CH-aware. (§6.4)
8. **Einzelunternehmen fixed.** `qual.owner_operated` fires on legal form as well as GF count. (§6.1)
9. **Idempotency hardened.** UNIQUE constraints, pre-call cost reservation, hash-keyed extraction. (§4, §5, §7)
10. **`company_profile` SQL view** added. (§4)
11. **`meta.description_length` dropped** from scoring. (§5.3)

---

## 1. Purpose

A locally-run tool that discovers candidate companies in the DACH region, gathers **evidence** about the state of their content marketing from public sources, assigns a reproducible priority score, and presents a reviewable list.

The output of a good run is not "here are 200 emails." It is: *"Schulz Manufaktur GmbH, Shopware shop, owner-operated, last blog post March 2023, no Article schema, invisible in AI answers for its own category — priority A."*

Every number in the score must trace back to a stored artifact with a URL and a fetch timestamp.

The exported research brief per company is a first-contact asset: it must read like the KI-Sichtbarkeits-Baseline-Report format that has already worked in live pitches — concrete findings, evidenced, in German.

## 2. Non-goals

Explicitly out of scope. Do not implement these, and do not let scope creep add them:

- **No email sending.** Not a feature, not a stub, not "for later." See §8.
- **No LinkedIn, Xing, or Instagram scraping.** ToS violation, actively blocked, not worth the engineering.
- **No multi-user support, auth, or deployment.** Single operator, localhost, SQLite.
- **No CRM.** Outreach tracking is a single flat table, nothing more.
- **No paid contact database integration** (Apollo, ZoomInfo, Cognism). The Impressum is a better and cheaper source for DACH.
- **No ScrapeGraphAI or other LLM-framework dependency.** Two fixed-schema extractions do not justify a framework. Direct SDK calls only.

## 3. Architecture

```
                  ┌──────────────┐
  Places API ───▶ │  discovery   │ ──▶ company (domain, name, city)
  Seed CSV   ───▶ └──────────────┘
                         │
                         ▼
                  ┌──────────────┐
                  │    fetch     │ ──▶ artifact (raw HTML/XML, hashed)
                  └──────────────┘     robots.txt → homepage → sitemap.xml
                         │              → impressum → blog index → product page
                         ▼
                  ┌──────────────┐
                  │  extract P1  │ ──▶ deterministic signals (free)
                  └──────────────┘
                         │
                         ▼
                  ┌──────────────┐
                  │  score  P1   │ ──▶ provisional band + gate decision
                  └──────────────┘
                         │  phase1_total + remaining_upside >= B floor
                         ▼
                  ┌──────────────┐     PageSpeed API
                  │  extract P2  │ ──▶ Impressum + homepage extraction (Anthropic SDK, Batch)
                  └──────────────┘     AI-visibility check (Anthropic SDK + web search)
                         │
                         ▼
                  ┌──────────────┐
                  │  reconcile   │ ──▶ batch results → signals, actual cost
                  └──────────────┘
                         │
                         ▼
                  ┌──────────────┐
                  │  score  P2   │ ──▶ final score + score_component
                  └──────────────┘     (rule_id, points, letter-ready German reason)
                         │
                         ▼
                  FastAPI + HTMX UI  (localhost:8000)
```

**Stack:** Python 3.11+, FastAPI, SQLite (WAL mode), `httpx`, `selectolax` for parsing, `anthropic` SDK, Jinja2 + HTMX for the UI, and `uvicorn` **from M4 only** — FastAPI is a framework and cannot serve itself, so `portal serve` (§9) needs an ASGI server. It is listed here so it is not mistaken for scope creep later. No build step, no Node, no Docker.

The list is closed. Anything not named here is a decision to be taken deliberately, not a convenience import — see M1.3 above, where a security concern was answered inside the stack rather than by adding to it.

**Repo layout:** single self-contained repository. No fork of any scraping framework exists or is needed.

## 4. Database schema

SQLite. Migrations via plain numbered `.sql` files applied in order; no ORM migration framework.

```sql
-- ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- Core entity
-- ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
CREATE TABLE company (
    id              INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    domain          TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,      -- normalised: lowercase, no scheme, no www
    legal_name      TEXT,
    legal_form      TEXT,                      -- 'GmbH'|'GmbH & Co. KG'|'e.K.'|'Einzelunternehmen'|'GbR'|'AG'|'UG'|…
    city            TEXT,
    postal_code     TEXT,
    country         TEXT CHECK (country IN ('DE','AT','CH')),
    discovery_source TEXT NOT NULL,            -- 'places' | 'seed_csv' | 'manual'
    discovery_query TEXT,                      -- the query that surfaced it, for provenance
    discovered_at   TEXT NOT NULL,             -- ISO8601 UTC
    excluded        INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    excluded_reason TEXT,                      -- never exclude silently; always record why
    needs_review    INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, -- derived: 1 iff an unresolved review_flag exists.
                                                -- Maintained by trigger, never written directly.
    contact_blocked INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 -- derived: 1 iff an unresolved review_flag names a
                                               -- reason in contact_blocking_reason (migration 008).
                                               -- A cache for the UI; the refusal itself is a trigger
                                               -- on `outreach` reading the flags live. See §8, A7.
);
CREATE INDEX idx_company_excluded ON company(excluded);
CREATE INDEX idx_company_review ON company(needs_review);

-- ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- B2: soft review flags. §6.4's three reasons are independent and
-- can co-occur, so one row per (company, reason) rather than one
-- TEXT column shared by three writers in three different stages.
--
-- Raising a flag is idempotent, so `score --phase 1` stays a zero-cost
-- repeatable recompute (§5.4). The idiom is a targeted DO NOTHING:
--
--   INSERT INTO review_flag (company_id, reason, raised_run_id, raised_at)
--   VALUES (?,?,?,?) ON CONFLICT (company_id, reason) DO NOTHING;
--
-- NOT `INSERT OR IGNORE`, which suppresses CHECK violations as well as
-- uniqueness conflicts — a renamed or misspelled reason would then be
-- dropped silently rather than raising, which is the opposite of the
-- fail-loudly rule the CHECK is there to enforce.
--
-- Resolution is sticky — see §6.4, which states the rule and the
-- reasoning. The unique index is what implements it; §6.4 is what
-- decides it.
-- ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
CREATE TABLE review_flag (
    id            INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    company_id    INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES company(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    reason        TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (reason IN (
                      'no_impressum','possible_marketplace_only','blog_date_unparseable')),
    raised_run_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES run(id),
    raised_at     TEXT NOT NULL,
    resolved_at   TEXT,                        -- NULL = not yet reviewed
    resolved_by_human INTEGER CHECK (resolved_by_human IN (0,1)),
                                               -- 1 = a human dismissed it; 0 = the pipeline cleared it.
                                               -- NULL passes: a SQLite CHECK holds unless it evaluates
                                               -- false, and the paired CHECK below governs the NULL case.
    resolved_note TEXT,
    CHECK ((resolved_at IS NULL     AND resolved_by_human IS NULL)
        OR (resolved_at IS NOT NULL AND resolved_by_human IS NOT NULL))
);
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_review_flag ON review_flag(company_id, reason);
CREATE INDEX idx_review_flag_open ON review_flag(company_id) WHERE resolved_at IS NULL;

-- `company.needs_review` is a cache of "has an unresolved flag", kept
-- correct by trigger so there is exactly one write path: write the flag,
-- the boolean follows. §9's filter stays an indexed column lookup rather
-- than an EXISTS subquery on every page load.
-- All three correlate the subquery to `company.id` rather than to NEW/OLD, so
-- the UPDATE trigger can recompute both sides with one statement: an update
-- that moved a flag between companies would otherwise leave the company it
-- left behind still marked.
CREATE TRIGGER trg_review_flag_after_insert AFTER INSERT ON review_flag
BEGIN
    UPDATE company SET needs_review = EXISTS (
        SELECT 1 FROM review_flag WHERE company_id = company.id AND resolved_at IS NULL
    ) WHERE id = NEW.company_id;
END;

CREATE TRIGGER trg_review_flag_after_update AFTER UPDATE ON review_flag
BEGIN
    UPDATE company SET needs_review = EXISTS (
        SELECT 1 FROM review_flag WHERE company_id = company.id AND resolved_at IS NULL
    ) WHERE id IN (OLD.company_id, NEW.company_id);
END;

CREATE TRIGGER trg_review_flag_after_delete AFTER DELETE ON review_flag
BEGIN
    UPDATE company SET needs_review = EXISTS (
        SELECT 1 FROM review_flag WHERE company_id = company.id AND resolved_at IS NULL
    ) WHERE id = OLD.company_id;
END;

-- ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- Raw fetched pages. Kept so re-scoring never costs a refetch.
-- D5(b): fetched_at = first seen; last_checked_at = most recent verification.
-- On a pre-existing database this column arrives as:
--   ALTER TABLE artifact ADD COLUMN last_checked_at TEXT;
-- ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
CREATE TABLE artifact (
    id              INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    company_id      INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES company(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    kind            TEXT NOT NULL,             -- 'robots'|'homepage'|'sitemap'|'impressum'|'blog_index'|'product_page'
    url             TEXT NOT NULL,
    http_status     INTEGER,
    content_hash    TEXT,                      -- sha256 of body; extraction is keyed to this
    body_path       TEXT,                      -- relative path on disk; bodies are NOT stored in SQLite
    bytes           INTEGER,
    fetched_at      TEXT NOT NULL,             -- first time this exact content was seen
    last_checked_at TEXT,                      -- most recent time this URL was verified
    error           TEXT
);
CREATE INDEX idx_artifact_company_kind ON artifact(company_id, kind);
-- Idempotency: a re-fetch of identical content must not create a second row.
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_artifact_identity ON artifact(company_id, kind, content_hash)
    WHERE content_hash IS NOT NULL;
```

**D5(b) — all artifact writes use this upsert.** With `INSERT OR IGNORE`, an unchanged page never updated `fetched_at`, so "when did I last check this" was unanswerable — and the 30-day PageSpeed cache rule in §5.3 depends on exactly that.

```sql
INSERT INTO artifact (company_id, kind, url, http_status, content_hash, body_path, bytes, fetched_at, last_checked_at)
VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?)
ON CONFLICT (company_id, kind, content_hash) WHERE content_hash IS NOT NULL DO UPDATE
SET last_checked_at = excluded.last_checked_at,
    http_status     = excluded.http_status;
```

> The `WHERE content_hash IS NOT NULL` on the conflict target is not optional and is not decoration. `uq_artifact_identity` is a **partial** index, and SQLite matches a conflict target to a partial index only when the predicate is repeated verbatim. Without it every artifact write raises `ON CONFLICT clause does not match any PRIMARY KEY or UNIQUE constraint`. Found in M1; v0.3's original snippet omitted it and could never have run.

```sql
-- ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- Signals: append-only, one row per observation, always evidenced.
-- ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
CREATE TABLE signal (
    id            INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    company_id    INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES company(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    run_id        INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES run(id),
    key           TEXT NOT NULL,               -- see §6 for the controlled vocabulary
    value_text    TEXT,
    value_num     REAL,
    value_date    TEXT,
    method        TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (method IN ('deterministic','llm')),
    confidence    REAL,                        -- NULL for deterministic; 0-1 for llm
    evidence_url  TEXT NOT NULL,               -- the page this was read off
    artifact_id   INTEGER REFERENCES artifact(id),
    observed_at   TEXT NOT NULL
);
CREATE INDEX idx_signal_company_key ON signal(company_id, key);
-- Idempotency: re-running a crashed extract stage must not duplicate observations
-- within the same run. See §5 (D6) for what this does and does not guarantee
-- across run boundaries.
--
-- M1.5 — every write to signal uses this idiom:
--
--   INSERT INTO signal (…) VALUES (…)
--   ON CONFLICT (run_id, company_id, key, evidence_url) DO NOTHING;
--
-- NOT `INSERT OR IGNORE`. `OR IGNORE` suppresses the CHECK on `method` as well
-- as the uniqueness conflict, so a typo'd or renamed method would be dropped in
-- silence — a signal that was never written, indistinguishable from one that
-- was never observed. The targeted DO NOTHING dedupes and nothing more. This is
-- the same trap review_flag avoids, for the same reason.
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_signal_identity ON signal(run_id, company_id, key, evidence_url);

-- ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- Wide read model for scoring. A VIEW, not a table: no sync, no
-- second write path. Pivots the LATEST observation per key.
-- Add a column here when a new signal key enters the scoring rules;
-- keys not listed remain queryable via the signal table.
--
-- M1.36: "latest per key" is scoped to the latest run of that key's OWN
-- STAGE, per company — see migration 006, which supersedes the definition
-- below. A later run of a stage is authoritative for everything that stage
-- owns, INCLUDING the keys it deliberately did not write. Without that,
-- a signal a stage stops writing is never retracted, and every A7 guard —
-- all of which work by declining to write — is undone by the read model.
--
-- M1.39: and the run must have FINISHED (migration 007). Authority by
-- omission is only sound from a run that reached the end: a crash mints a
-- new run_id (§5, D6), so a partial pass would otherwise retract every key
-- it never got to. A run still in flight, one that crashed, and one that
-- aborted are all the same thing here — an account nobody has claimed is
-- complete — and each is ignored WHOLESALE, keys it did write included.
--
-- D5(a): the ORDER BY carries an `id DESC` tiebreaker. Signals written
-- within the same second share an observed_at; without the tiebreaker,
-- which one the view surfaces is arbitrary and can differ between
-- queries — contradicting the reproducibility guarantee the tool rests on.
--
-- A2 (M1.76, migration 011): the Phase-2 columns arrive — `own_brand`,
-- `homepage_extracted`, `one_line_offer`, and `legal_form` COALESCEd over
-- the LLM's value so §5.5b's "the LLM wins on disagreement" is ONE
-- EXPRESSION in the read model rather than a race between two UPDATEs.
--
-- A4 (M1.79, migration 012): the pivot now carries a CONFIDENCE PREDICATE.
-- `confidence = 0` is §5.5b's record of a value the tool VERIFIED AND DOES
-- NOT BELIEVE, and until M5 no writer could produce one, so the view had
-- never needed the filter. M5 is what makes confidence ≠ 1 possible, so the
-- filter lands before the first Phase-2 write and not after it.
-- ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
CREATE VIEW company_profile AS
WITH latest AS (
    SELECT s.*,
           ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY company_id, key
                              ORDER BY observed_at DESC, id DESC) AS rn
    FROM signal s
)
SELECT
    c.id AS company_id,
    c.domain,
    c.country,
    c.legal_form,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='platform.detected'          THEN l.value_text END) AS platform,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='content.blog_last_post'     THEN l.value_date END) AS blog_last_post,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='content.blog_post_count'    THEN l.value_num  END) AS blog_post_count,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='content.blog_exists'        THEN l.value_num  END) AS blog_exists,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='schema.article_present'     THEN l.value_num  END) AS article_schema,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='schema.product_present'     THEN l.value_num  END) AS product_schema,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='i18n.hreflang_count'        THEN l.value_num  END) AS hreflang_count,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='perf.lighthouse_performance' THEN l.value_num END) AS lighthouse_perf,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='agency.footer_credit'       THEN l.value_text END) AS agency_credit,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='catalog.product_url_count'  THEN l.value_num  END) AS product_url_count,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='reviews.count'              THEN l.value_num  END) AS review_count,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='reviews.trusted_shops'      THEN l.value_num  END) AS trusted_shops,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='impressum.gf_count'         THEN l.value_num  END) AS gf_count,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='impressum.owner_named'      THEN l.value_num  END) AS owner_named,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='ai.brand_mentions'          THEN l.value_num  END) AS ai_brand_mentions,
    MAX(CASE WHEN l.key='ai.queries_checked'         THEN l.value_num  END) AS ai_queries_checked
FROM company c
LEFT JOIN latest l ON l.company_id = c.id AND l.rn = 1
GROUP BY c.id;
```

**A4 — the confidence predicate, and which way it fails (M1.79, migration 012).**

§5.5b already rules that a value whose evidence is not on the page is *"discarded and the signal written with `confidence=0` for review"*. **`discarded` had no implementation.** The read model pivoted latest-per-key with no `confidence` predicate anywhere in it, so the row §5.5b writes to say *the tool does not believe this* would have been served to `evaluate` as though it did. Measured on the stored corpus before the change: **2,404 signal rows, every one `method='deterministic'` with `confidence IS NULL`** — so the filter is a no-op today and is being added at the only moment it can be added without a migration that also has to repair data. M5 is what makes `confidence ≠ 1` possible at all.

The predicate is `confidence IS NULL OR confidence > 0`:

- **`NULL` passes** — §4's `signal.confidence` is *"NULL for deterministic; 0-1 for llm"*, so excluding NULL would blank Phase 1 entirely.
- **`0` is excluded, and no threshold above 0 is invented.** §5.5b uses exactly one confidence value to mean *rejected*, and a plausibility threshold chosen on zero observations is M1.4's error — the same one §10.3 refuses for *"fewer than N products is suspicious"*.

**Where the filter goes is load-bearing, and the obvious place is wrong.** It must be applied **after** `current_run` has chosen the authoritative run, never before. Filtering inside `observed` would remove a Phase-2 run's unverified rows from the set `current_run` picks its `MAX(run_id)` from — so a run whose extractions all failed verification would not be its stage's authoritative run, and an *older* run's values would be served as current. That is M1.36 exactly: the defect migration 006 exists to close, re-created by the guard meant to strengthen it.

**Direction of error: too strict, and it says so.** A filtered value is a signal the scoring model does not see, so the rule that reads it declines or abstains. **That is uniformly the too-low direction, and it is checkable rather than hopeful**: every key §5.5b writes that any §6 rule reads feeds a rule that *awards* points — `impressum.legal_form` and `impressum.gf_count` and `site.owner_named` at +15, `brand.own_brand` at +10 — and the one Phase-2 key on a rule that *subtracts* is `agency.footer_credit_llm`, which A3 gives no reader at all. So the filter cannot withhold a penalty, and therefore cannot push a company toward a phone call. The opposite choice — no filter — scores a company on a value the tool measured and rejected, which is the direction §8 fails an export for.

**And the loss is visible rather than silent**, which is the condition A7 attaches to every abstention: the rejected row stays in `signal` and is queryable; §9 renders `confidence = 0` red on the row expansion; `llm.impressum_extracted` / `llm.homepage_extracted` are written with `confidence = 1` and therefore survive the filter, so the read model can still distinguish *the extraction ran and its answer was rejected* from *Phase 2 never ran here*. Without those two stage facts the filter would convert a rejection into a silence, and the three-state predicates in §6.1 would have nothing to read.

**C4 — `uq_signal_identity` includes `evidence_url`. DECIDED: it stays, and here is what the duplicate costs (M1.80).**

The concern is that `(run_id, company_id, key, evidence_url)` lets one key be written twice within a run under two URLs, so a redirect defeats the dedup that `ON CONFLICT … DO NOTHING` is supposed to provide. Three things were measured or derived before deciding, rather than after:

1. **The column has never disambiguated anything.** Over the whole stored corpus: **2,404 signal rows and 2,404 distinct `(run_id, company_id, key)` triples — zero groups with more than one `evidence_url`.** Dropping `evidence_url` from the index today would change no row in the database.
2. **`evidence_url` is not a live URL and cannot be redirected.** It is `artifact.url`, taken off the stored artifact row in the same expression as `artifact_id` (M1.42, `extract._write`, and M5's writer is held to the same signature). A redirect happens in `fetch` and is frozen into the artifact row before any extraction runs, so **the case the finding describes cannot arise in an extraction stage at all** — including M5's, which is the first stage to write signals whose evidence is a URL that *was* redirected on the way in. §5.6's dedup guarantee depends on the second invocation computing the same `evidence_url`, and it does, because it reads it from a row that does not move.
3. **What a duplicate would cost if one ever arose: not a score.** `company_profile` pivots `rn = 1` with a deterministic `(observed_at DESC, id DESC)` tiebreaker (D5(a)), so exactly one value surfaces and it is the same one on every query. It costs §9 a second evidence link in the row expansion, and it narrows §5's D6 sentence from *"no duplicate observations within the same run"* to *"no duplicate observations from the same document within the same run"* — which is what the index has always actually guaranteed.
