Not popped, not applied**, because this is a register correction and not a recovery: **9a rebuilt most of the content independently**, and the comparison is worth more than the file — `verify.py` exists in the tree today at 6,053 bytes, structurally identical in its ruling (*the verifier takes the sent text as an argument and cannot reach an artifact*) and written in different prose, which is the strongest available evidence that the ruling is right. **What is genuinely still unbuilt is now knowable rather than guessed**: as measured on 2026-08-21, `portal/pagespeed.py` had never been rebuilt and `pagespeed` appeared nowhere in the tree, and of migration `010`'s five changes only `contact.vat_id` had a writer, `run.pagespeed_calls` existed nowhere, and `llm_batch.status = 'balance_exhausted'` was §10.6's *ahead of its writer*. Migration `010`'s number was taken by `score.evaluated_on` (M1.74) and **the stashed file cannot reclaim it**; its content, if wanted, arrives on `016` or later with its writers, per M1.45(c).

  **Superseded by the build, 2026-09-03 (M1.101).** `portal/pagespeed.py` and `run.pagespeed_calls` **now exist**, on migration `016`, written from §5.3 and §5.5a rather than from the stash — which stays exactly where it is, unread, on a machine this container cannot reach. `balance_exhausted` shipped earlier still, in migration `014`'s CHECK constraint with `reconcile` as its writer. **The paragraph above is left standing as the measurement it was**, dated and superseded rather than edited away: a register that silently rewrites its own past readings cannot be audited, which is the whole of M1.97. What must not survive is the *unqualified present tense* — "exists nowhere" was true of one tree on one day, and this is the sentence M1.98 was written about.

- **The corpus is DISPOSABLE, and its recovery procedure is written down (M1.96).** `data/portal.db` — 137 MB, the 13-company corpus — was destroyed on 2026-08-20 and this section had never said what it was worth. **The ruling rests on the fact that the irreplaceable input is already durable**: `seeds/candidates.csv` is committed, and what was lost is derived data that `fetch` and `extract-p1` recompute at no monetary cost. **Recovery:** `portal init` → `portal fetch --seed seeds/candidates.csv` → `portal extract-p1` → `portal score --phase 1`. The accepted cost is wall-clock at the §5.2 floor (1 req/s per host, 2 hosts in flight) and **zero LLM spend** — Phase 2 has never run against it. **DURABLE was rejected on §8**, not on convenience: an off-container copy of a database naming 13 real businesses sits outside the ruling M3 already took, on behalf of people who did not consent to the first copy. **A re-crawl produces A corpus, not THE corpus** — every measurement taken against the lost one is unreproducible in principle, and §10.7 is the register of them.

- **M3 — the repository names 13 real prospects. The decision is to MAKE THE REPOSITORY PRIVATE**: not pseudonymise, not gitignore the seeds. The review's own analysis rules out both alternatives — the findings documents' worth is inseparable from the named sites, so pseudonymising destroys the asset, and gitignoring `candidates.csv` while leaving the named documents in place is the wrong half. **One fact the review did not state:** the repository publishes §6's **scoring weights and band thresholds alongside the named prospects and assessments of their marketing**, so any named company can compute exactly why it scored low. That is the difference between a list of leads and a published critique with the marking scheme attached. **DONE. The operator made the change, and this line's own condition is what closed it.** It read *"this line stays until the command returns `PRIVATE`"* through five units, each of which re-ran the command and got `PUBLIC` — Unit 4 and Unit 5 on 2026-08-17, Units 6, 7 and 8 after. **Unit 9a re-ran it on 2026-08-20 and it returns `{"isPrivate": true, "visibility": "PRIVATE"}`.** The exposure is over. **Kept rather than deleted, because the shape is the lesson**: the row survived five units without drifting, being wished closed, or being carried forward as a summary of itself, for exactly one reason — its state was a *command*, not a sentence. That is M1.73's fix arrived at independently, and it is the second counter §Unit 2a records as *not* having drifted. The thing that made it work was that nobody could report on it without measuring it.

- **M1 (§7's cost ceiling exists in the spec and nowhere in the code) is correct. IT LANDED AS UNIT 7, ON ITS OWN, BEFORE M5 — this bullet previously said "WITH M5" and that was the wrong call.** Two reasons, and they are the ones that separated 6a from 6b. A defect in the ledger must not be attributable to M5's writer code, and **the ledger needs no API key while M5 does** — so the ledger is testable under the CI that M1.65 built, which forbids `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, and M5's reservation caller is not. Sequencing it after M5 would also have meant M5 was written against the ledger's *absence*, which is how a bypass becomes load-bearing before anyone notices it exists.

  **Two of the three sub-tasks were mis-stated here and are corrected in §7 rather than carried forward.** (a) The claim that the schema defines **"no month boundary and no query"** was **false when written (M1.70)**: §7 control 2 already specified a rolling 30-day window *and* carried the literal SQL. Nothing needed deciding; this bullet invited the boundary to be re-derived, and re-deriving a decision §7 had already taken is the error this whole section exists to prevent. (b) The instruction to write the ledger query over `run.est_cost_usd` **and** `llm_batch.est_cost_usd`/`actual_cost_usd` is a **double-count (M1.69)**, because control 4 reserves into both tables; §7 control 2's own draft query sums `run` alone and is the correct one. (c) The import-time assertion is the part that was right, and it is the part that made this a mechanism rather than a convention — see M1.71.

  **What is left for M5 is the two controls that need a caller**: control 3's per-run reservation and control 4's batch reservation, the second of which must make its two writes one transaction (M1.72).

- **H2 (the redirect SSRF guard) and L1 (extracting `SitePolicies`) are Unit 6. BOTH LANDED, 2026-08-18 — L1 first (M1.67), then H2 on top of it (M1.68).** The heading order in the review reads H2-then-L1 and its body says the opposite; the body is right and is what was done. The review is right that the address guard belongs in that seam. Combining a security behaviour change with a 286-line extraction means a defect in either is attributable to both — and M1.42 is already this project's lesson about a second expression describing what the first one did. Extraction lands first as a pure no-behaviour-change commit; the guard lands on top of it.

### 10.5 Undecided, not blocking

- **`content.blog_last_post` is a lower bound, not the last post date (M1.30).** The index's date is a maximum over the posts it *lists*, the sampled article's is one post's, and the later of the two is better than either — but neither is guaranteed to be the newest post on the blog. `opp.blog_stale` awards **+20** for *not* publishing, so an under-estimate of freshness fires it wrongly, which is the expensive direction. (`+25` is `opp.no_blog`; the paragraph's argument is about the size of the expensive direction, so the number matters.) The available fix is to select the sample by the **newest `<lastmod>` in the blog sitemap shard** and still read `datePublished` off the page — lastmod as the *selector*, never as the *value*, so its known freshness bias cannot reach the signal (at worst it picks a recently edited old post). That changes A6's ordering, which was ratified as shallowest-first, so it is recorded here rather than taken. **Now written up as a tier hierarchy — see `docs/blog-article-selector-proposal.md`, which measures it on the corpus and is the thing to rule on.** It proposes two tiers, newest-`lastmod` then A6-as-ratified. A third — first article in index document order — was measured and dropped: every confirmation of the newest-first convention comes from an index that dates its posts, which is precisely the population where no sample is needed, so on the cases that matter the convention is unfalsifiable by observation rather than merely unobserved.

  **What has been taken, meanwhile, is the interim guard (M1.32).** It is deliberately independent of the question above: `content.blog_last_post_basis` records whether the date rests on the index (a maximum over listed posts) or on the sample alone (a floor with nothing above it), and §6.2's two staleness rungs decline to fire on the latter. That closes the expensive direction on the 2 shops where it is open today and constrains nothing about how the sample is later chosen. It is **not** retired by the selector fix: a lastmod-chosen sample is a better lower bound, not a maximum, so `basis = article` stays unbounded whatever picks the article. Retiring it needs a source that bounds the newest post from above, which the proposal is explicit about not being.

  **Resolved: a suppressed rung routes to a human.** `blog_date_unbounded` (§6.4, migration 004) is raised by `score` wherever the guard silences a staleness rung, carrying the lower bound in `raised_note`. It is a distinct reason rather than a reuse of `catalog_not_measurable`, because *a value that cannot bound the rule* and *no value at all* send a person to do different things. The general form is now A7 (§5), which this was the fourth instance of.
- **`neg.active_content`'s abstention had no routing, and it was the one that most needed it (M1.37, A7 instance 7). Resolved 2026-08-16.** `blog_cadence_unmeasurable` (§6.4, migration 008), plus the consequence the direction of the error demands: a too-high abstention **blocks outbound contact** until a human resolves it, the same way §8 fails an export that cannot state its basis. The general form is A7's **third axis** (§5), which this instance forced into the tables: *which way the score is wrong while it waits* is not the same question as *would running again help*, and only the first of the two had ever been recorded. On the corpus it now abstains on 6 of 13 and blocks all six.
- **The gate treats an abstained Phase-1 rule as zero upside (M3).** `remaining_upside` counts Phase-2 reachability only, so a company can be stopped on a total the pipeline knows is incomplete — `propellerdiscount.de`, at 0 + 50 against a B floor of 55, with an abstained blog ladder worth up to +25. §5.4's safety claim is that nothing whose final score could reach B is discarded, and for one run that claim rests on an unmeasured rung. It self-corrects on the next run, since scoring is a free recompute and no Phase-2 money has been spent. Whether an abstained **transient** should carry its points into the bound is a change to a gate ratified on 2026-08-16, so it is recorded here rather than taken.
- **A persistent transient needed a routing (M1.34, A7b). Resolved 2026-08-16.** `fetch_persistently_failing` (§6.4, migration 009), counted by `score` over consecutive scoring runs at the N = 3 / 3-distinct-days policy A7 already bound. One reason for all three instances — the product sample, the blog index and the sampled article — because they send a person to one question, and `raised_note` carries which URL. It had waited a milestone: for three runs a company could have a rule going quiet with nobody told.
- **Should `neg.active_content`'s −25 step, or decay? (M1.63, opened 2026-08-17.)** Observed once, on `navucko.com`: a single post crossing the 180-day line took the largest penalty in §6.3 off a lead overnight and moved it D → C, with no change to the site and none to the code. The rule is behaving as specified — `>= 4` dated posts inside six months, both numbers hard — and the question is whether a step function is the right shape for "publishes regularly" when the input is a lower bound that decays continuously. **Not taken**, because it is a §6.3 weight question and §10.3 already forbids calibrating on this corpus; recorded so the next instance is recognised rather than re-derived. The measurement is in `docs/unit4-robots-tristate-findings.md` §7.
- **Timestamps are read as UTC dates.** `navucko.com`'s newest post moved 2026-06-21 → 2026-06-20 when M1.31 made timestamps parseable: `…T22:00:00Z` is the 21st in CEST. Immaterial at §6.2's month-scale thresholds and recorded so it is not later mistaken for drift.
- **M6 is unblocked, and the thing that unblocked it also moved the cost (M1.54).** The brief blocked M6 on the D4 pricing confirmation and `docs/v0.3-review-findings.md` added a second condition — *does Haiku 4.5 support web search at all?* Both are now answered: $10/1,000 searches (§5.5c, 2026-08-15) and yes, via the basic `web_search_20250305` variant. The consequence to price into §7 when M6 is scheduled is that the **newer** `web_search_20260209` — which filters results with code execution before they reach the context window — requires Opus 4.6+ / Sonnet 4.6+, so on Haiku 4.5 raw results land in context in full and tokens per search exceed what the $10/1,000 figure implies. §5.5c's 10–20k input tokens per query was estimated on exactly this behaviour; what changes is that it cannot be assumed to fall later, because the cheaper-in-context tool is not available on this model. **Unblocked, not started** — until 2026-09-03: **built as Unit 10 (M1.105)**, with the consequence priced exactly as this bullet said it should be (`ai_visibility.SEARCH_CONTEXT_TOKENS`, dated).
- **The address guard resolves, and then httpx resolves again (M1.68's stated residual). UNOBSERVED, labelled rather than fixed.** `AddressPolicy.verdict_for` asks the resolver where a name points, and httpcore asks again when it opens the socket. A resolver under an attacker's control can answer differently the second time — classic DNS rebinding — and the guard would have approved an address the client never connected to. Three things bound it, and the third is why it is not being built today. **(a)** The case that matters most is immune: an IP literal (`http://169.254.169.254/`, `http://127.0.0.1:8009/`) is judged without consulting DNS at all, so the metadata and own-machine attacks cannot be reached this way. **(b)** It requires the attacker to run authoritative DNS for the redirect target *and* to win a race against an OS resolver cache, which is a materially higher bar than "own a shop that redirects". **(c)** Closing it means **pinning**: resolve once, connect to that literal address, and carry the original hostname in `Host:` and in TLS SNI and certificate verification — a custom `httpx` transport, which is real production surface added for a threat this project has never observed, and M1.4 is the standing lesson about building against an unobserved case. **Take it when a seeded domain is ever observed resolving inconsistently, or when the tool is pointed at anything other than a hand-curated seed list.** Recorded here so that the next person to read the guard does not conclude it is airtight.
- **The guard classifies addresses, not networks it cannot see.** A shop hosted on a public address that proxies to something internal is not covered by anything here, and cannot be — that is the target's own architecture. Named so the guard's claim is not read wider than it is.
- Ollama for local extraction instead of Haiku — saves ~$10/month at Phase-2 volumes, costs German-language extraction quality and the substring-verification simplicity. Currently: use Haiku.
- Whether to store artifact bodies compressed (gzip) — likely yes above a few hundred companies.
- Research brief export stays Markdown for v1; DOCX (letter-ready) is a candidate for v1.1 once the brief content has stabilised against real outreach feedback.
- Band thresholds (§6.5) are provisional pending the first 100-company calibration run.

### 10.7 Unverifiable — recorded as such rather than as a value

**This section exists because a project that numbers its measurements has to be able to say when one has lost its evidence.** Both halves below are M1.59's tri-state applied to this project's own audit trail: *we cannot tell* and *there is nothing* are different states, they send a reader to different places, and collapsing the first into the second is how a gap becomes a fact. Nothing here is deleted and nothing here is restated as though still checkable.

#### 10.7a Measurements taken against the destroyed corpus (M1.96, M1.97)

`data/portal.db` was destroyed on **2026-08-20** (M1.95). §10.4b rules the corpus **disposable** and gives the re-crawl procedure — but **a re-crawl produces A corpus, not THE corpus.** The 13 sites have moved on: posts have been published, sitemaps regenerated, robots files edited, shop systems upgraded. Every measurement below was true of the database that no longer exists, on the date given.

**The marker is the literal string `MEASURED <date>, NOT REPRODUCIBLE`**, greppable, carried here and not scattered as prose. **The dates differ by five days and that is deliberate** — one "the lost corpus" date would be exactly the copied scalar M1.73 is about.

| # | Measurement | Where it is cited | Taken |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **3 of 13** shops cannot earn up to 25 points on URL structure alone (`opulent-wohnen.com`, `smoke2u.de`, `verpackungskoenig.de`) | §10.3 — the reason §6.5 may not be calibrated | MEASURED 2026-08-15, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 2 | `smile-store.de` reports **194** product URLs from Tier 1, having left the group in row 1 | §10.3, M1.24 | MEASURED 2026-08-15, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 3 | **5 of 12** are plainly owner-operated sole traders whose legal form is unstated, and the predicate matches **none** — the basis for §7.1's **$31–36/month** steady state | §10.2, §7.1 | MEASURED 2026-08-15, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 4 | `Inh.`/`Inhaber` appears on **1 of 11** stored Impressum pages in `visible_text` and **3 of 12** across raw HTML | §10.2 — the base rate §10.2 will be settled on | MEASURED 2026-08-16, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 5 | `Amtsgericht` re-measured at **3/12** against a recorded **2/12**, a disagreement that cannot be adjudicated | M1.48 | MEASURED 2026-08-16, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 6 | Feed autodiscovery fires on **4 of the 6**, and all four are platform defaults | §10.1's rejected-instrument table, M1.14/M1.34 | MEASURED 2026-08-16, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 7 | `neg.active_content` abstains on **6 of 13** and blocks outbound contact on all six | §10.5, M1.37 | MEASURED 2026-08-16, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 8 | The stale read model cost **three JTL shops −20** each, and `zecplus.de` carried `content.blog_exists = 0` from a pre-M1.34 run (**+25**) | M1.36 | MEASURED 2026-08-16, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 9 | `propellerdiscount.de` stopped at **0 + 50** against a B floor of **55**, with an abstained blog ladder worth up to +25 | §10.5's gate finding (M3) | MEASURED 2026-08-16, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 10 | The `/sitemap/product/N` shape appears on **0 of 307** stored sitemap artifacts | M1.55 | MEASURED 2026-08-17, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 11 | The corpus-wide disallow count is **exactly two** — artifacts **171 and 186** — under all three selection policies | M1.59/M1.60's robots tri-state | MEASURED 2026-08-17, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 12 | `navucko.com` moved **D → C** overnight on one post crossing the 180-day line, with no change to site or code | M1.63, §10.5 | MEASURED 2026-08-17, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 13 | `zecplus.de` has **29 bodies** (not 31) and artifact **458** is chosen over **1**; **26 bodies** on `www.smoke2u.de` and `www.propellerdiscount.de` name no robots row | M1.61 → M1.75, §10.4b precondition 2 | MEASURED 2026-08-18 at `641ff23`, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 14 | **2,404 signal rows**, every one `method='deterministic'` with `confidence IS NULL`, and 2,404 distinct `(run_id, company_id, key)` | M1.79, migration `012`'s premise | MEASURED 2026-08-20, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |
| 15 | `germanelectronic.de` is admitted to paid extraction at **5 + 50 = 55**, the B floor to the point, and 45 without `qual.own_brand` | M1.76 | MEASURED 2026-08-20, NOT REPRODUCIBLE |

**What this does and does not do to the findings above it.** It does **not** retract them: a finding does not become false when its evidence expires, and in every row above it was the *reasoning* that changed the code, not the count — M1.75 re-keyed the robots lookup because a permissive file from another origin governing a shop's bodies is wrong on its face, and it would still be wrong if the byte counts were different. What it does is remove these numbers from the set a later unit may treat as *checkable*. **A re-measurement after a re-crawl is a new measurement and takes a new date and a new row; it does not overwrite one of these.** Two numbers that describe different corpora under one citation is M1.42's two-expressions defect with a week between the expressions.

**Direction of error, stated because it is not symmetric.** A row marked unreproducible that could in fact be re-measured costs a later unit one re-measurement. An unmarked row that cannot be re-measured costs it a false correction that it will believe, and that will then propagate — which is M1.98's failure exactly, and this section is what M1.98 argues should have existed.

#### 10.7b CLOSED at zero, by measurement — whether a message batch was ever submitted (M1.100, M1.114)

**CLOSED on 2026-09-04. The answer is ZERO: no batch has ever been submitted on this account.** Measured by `portal llm-batches` against a real key for the billing account, which listed no batches and printed its terminal verdict line — *"§10.7b is CLOSED with the answer ZERO, as of 2026-09-04T00:39:17Z"* — at exit 0. **This is a measurement with a date on it, not an inference from the code.** The account history below is kept because the route to the answer is the finding, and because M1.114 turns on the difference between the two.

**9c is unblocked.** The precondition every unit since M1.100 has deferred to is met: there is no committed spend to account for, no `provider_batch_id` to write down, and nothing that a first `extract-p2 --submit` would double.

Every local instrument that could answer is gone or was never written. `llm_batch` lived only in `data/portal.db`; no code has ever written a row to it, because `extract-p2` stops short of §7 control 4's reservation by design (M1.83). The account-scoped listing that *would* answer has never been run: a session authorised on 2026-08-21 for exactly one read-only `messages.batches.list` found no API credential (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` unset, no `ant` CLI, no credential file) and reported the question open rather than reporting zero.

**The evidence against submission was strong and was all of one kind, and it was still not the answer.** `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` was unset in every session of every unit; CI's `assert-no-api-key` step fails the build if it is present (M1.65); the Unit 9c transcript records `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: NOT SET` at 04:34:20 on 2026-08-21, 89 seconds into the unit, before it stopped. But *no key on this machine* is a statement **about this machine** — which is precisely M1.98's defect, one day later and pointing the other way. **The measurement agreed with the inference, and the agreement is not what closed it: the account-scoped listing is.** Had they disagreed, the listing would have been right and every one of those facts would still have been true.

**Closing procedure, kept after the fact because it is the instrument, not the answer. AMENDED BY M1.112: the forms below replace the original pasted snippet, which could not tell *no batch* from *never asked*.** Run exactly as it was first written, on 2026-08-21 at 21:39 UTC, it printed **zero bytes on `stdout` and exited 1** — a `TypeError` during *client construction*, before any network call — and the procedure's stated reading was *"if it prints nothing, no batch exists"*. **A negative result must be a printed statement, never an absence of output.**

1. **`portal llm-batches` (M1.104) — the procedure as a command, and the form to prefer.** It ends every path with a terminal statement rather than with silence: no key is **exit 2** and *"§10.7b stays OPEN. This is not zero"*; an empty listing is *"§10.7b is CLOSED with the answer ZERO, as of \<date\>"*; a non-empty one prints every id and *"RESUBMITTING WOULD DOUBLE THE COST"*. It is classified **free** in the import-time guard (M1.71), needs a key, makes no paid call, and looks for no other credential (§7 control 9).

2. `messages.batches.list` on a machine holding a real API key for the billing account, **if the command is not available. The listing must end by printing a terminal verdict line; an empty output closes nothing:**
   ```
   python3 -c "
   import anthropic
   client = anthropic.Anthropic()   # raises here if there is no credential
   n = 0
   for b in client.messages.batches.list(limit=20):
       print(b.id, b.processing_status, b.created_at, b.request_counts)
       n += 1
   print(f'LISTING COMPLETED — {n} batch(es) on this account.')
   "
   ```
   **`LISTING COMPLETED` is the evidence, not the absence of rows.** If that line is not printed — for any reason, including a traceback on `stderr` that a caller reading `stdout` never sees — the call did not happen and **§10.7b stays open**. Check the exit status too; it is `0` only on a completed listing.

3. The Console's usage view for the same account, **read as a page that loaded**. The same rule applies: a view that did not render is not a view showing nothing.

**Direction of error, and it is the whole point: every form above can only fail toward *still open*.** A run that wrongly leaves the question open costs one re-run of a free, read-only call. A run that wrongly closes it authorises a resubmission against a batch that is already paid for. Those are not comparable costs.

**What the answer means, so it is not re-derived under time pressure.** A batch that exists means **submission happened and the spend is committed**, whether or not any result was ever read. Results stay retrievable for **29 days** from creation (§5.6); retrieving them costs **nothing extra**, because the money was spent at submission; **resubmitting would double the cost.** So this listing **precedes** any 9c work that could submit, and if a batch is found, its `provider_batch_id` must be written down before anything else — with `llm_batch` gone, that string is the only route back to the results.
